Message[1] - CHATGPT - 2026-08-25T04:13:38-0400 - I recommend five operational regimes: 危机/CRISIS, 防守/RISK_OFF, 观望/NEUTRAL, 布局/RISK_ON, and 顺势/TRENDING. CRISIS should be a hard-gate override with zero exposure; RISK_OFF should permit only reduced exposure; NEUTRAL should allow selective, strategy-qualified entries; RISK_ON should permit active deployment; and TRENDING should be a persistent bull-only leverage overlay for managing existing exposure, not a fresh-entry or high-return forecast. These regimes should describe market permission/risk capacity, while direction remains an orthogonal output (bull, neutral, or bear) used for strategy routing. That separation is important: a healthy bearish trend can support a short trend-following strategy in NEUTRAL or RISK_ON without being mislabeled as a bullish regime, and CRISIS remains direction-independent. The continuous MarketPermission score should carry the fine-grained exposure decision, with labels serving as human-readable bands: 0 for CRISIS, (0, 0.50) RISK_OFF, [0.50, 0.75) NEUTRAL, [0.75, 1.00) RISK_ON, and TRENDING as a separately qualified extension above 1.00 up to 1.40. I would not restore the older seven-state taxonomy: splitting CAUTION from ELEVATED_RISK adds apparent precision without a clearly distinct action, and CORRECTIVE confuses direction with permission. State transitions—especially CRISIS recovery and TRENDING entry/exit—must use persistence, hysteresis, and memory rather than independent daily thresholding.

Any allocation above 100% belongs in a downstream portfolio-sizing policy that consumes Condition, Impulse, Confidence, volatility, and portfolio constraints; it should not be embedded in the market-state engine. This resolves the unreachable/constant bonus bug structurally instead of choosing a new multiplier merely to make 1.40 attainable. NB37 validates the detector's descriptive episode selection, not the causal benefit or optimal magnitude of leverage, so it does not justify escalation by itself. Second, Breadth should remain an independent fourth pillar because participation is economically distinct from trend level, credit/risk appetite, and volatility/uncertainty, and it can deteriorate before a cap-weighted index loses trend. However, v4.4's exact Breadth construction and 15% weight are provisional, not inherited truth. Before production use we should (a) define a point-in-time-consistent universe or explicit pre-inception fallback for XLRE/XLC, (b) remove or re-derive TrendQuality's overlapping RSP/SPX concentration term so breadth is not double-counted, (c) re-estimate pillar weights out of sample, and then (d) recalibrate every composite-dependent gate and state boundary. Message[7] - CHATGPT - 2026-08-25T04:20:40-0400 - Take MemoryDiscount next. My recommendation is to remove it from the continuous measurement for the same architectural reason leverage was removed: current Condition should be a reproducible function of current and explicitly windowed market observations, not of an unobservable episode accumulator whose value depends on how far back the engine was initialized. Keep asymmetric state hysteresis and explicit CRISIS exit confirmation in the display/state machine; those mechanisms prevent label whipsaw without contaminating the underlying score. If a portfolio should restore exposure gradually after a crisis, implement a separately named RecoveryThrottle in the downstream sizing policy, where its half-life, reset rule, and interaction with volatility targeting can be tested against portfolio outcomes. Applying MemoryDiscount inside Condition creates three problems: it makes identical present-day pillars produce different scores based on path history; it can double-count stress already captured by Uncertainty, Risk Appetite, and crisis gates; and it makes Impulse falsely improve as the discount mechanically decays even if raw conditions are flat. Deriving Impulse from pre-memory composite fixes only the third symptom, not the first two. I would therefore formalize the layers as: raw pillars → hard data/market gates → capped Condition [0,1] → mutually exclusive state with asymmetric hysteresis and CRISIS confirmation; separately, Condition/state/Impulse/Confidence → portfolio policy, optionally including RecoveryThrottle and leverage. One naming implication follows: stop calling the measurement `MarketPermission` in the engine, because “permission” invites sizing policy back into the measurement layer; call it `Condition`, and let the portfolio layer emit `RiskBudget` or `ExposurePermission`. The only unresolved empirical question is whether a downstream RecoveryThrottle improves risk-adjusted outcomes; it is not a prerequisite for removing MemoryDiscount from Condition.

The best reproducible fallback for the full 2007+ research window is a fixed nine-ETF universe used unchanged for every date; it sacrifices the two later standalone sectors but preserves a stable denominator and avoids pretending duplicated parent-sector prices are independent observations. Run the full 11-ETF universe only as a separate post-inception robustness series over the common period, never as a continuation of the nine-sector history. The canonical production target should be point-in-time constituent breadth mapped to the contemporaneous GICS taxonomy, or authoritative historical sector-index series if obtainable; that is an empirical/licensing task. Therefore the data contract should declare tiers: Tier 1 canonical point-in-time sector/constituent data; Tier 2 fixed-nine ETF fallback for long history; Tier 3 fixed-eleven ETF diagnostic beginning only when all inputs satisfy warm-up; otherwise unavailable—no silent substitution. Every observation should carry universe version, source tier, denominator, coverage, warm-up status, and staleness, with Confidence penalized when a fallback is active. The A/B specification test should use the fixed-nine series across the long sample and repeat on fixed-eleven over the common sample to expose universe sensitivity. Compare at least: current Direction including RSP/SPX concentration plus Breadth; Direction without concentration plus Breadth; and Direction with concentration but no separate Breadth as the legacy control. Evaluate incremental predictive value, stability, crisis timing, transition churn, and subperiod robustness—not just in-sample fit. I agree the 74.3 TRENDING threshold must then be re-derived, but the reason is the changed feature definition and label objective, not merely removal of the leverage bonus. Optimize it for identifying persistent, low-volatility, low-drawdown bull episodes with acceptable coverage and false-positive rates; do not optimize it for maximum return. This gives us a concrete recommendation now while leaving the licensed-data upgrade path explicit.

The current v4.4 formula mixes two distinct concepts—pillar agreement and distance from a state boundary—and omits data quality; its historical percentile of cross-pillar dispersion is meaningful only after all four pillars share supportive-positive polarity. The superseding schema should publish four separately testable components: `pillar_agreement` (dispersion among Direction, Breadth, Risk Appetite, and Stability after renaming/inverting Uncertainty so higher always means more supportive); `data_completeness` (required observations present, fresh, warmed up, and on their declared source tier); `decision_margin` (distance from the actual applicable state decision surface, including TRENDING qualification/veto margins rather than only scalar band boundaries); and `temporal_stability` (recent Condition noise and label-transition fragility). Do not impute a missing required pillar as neutral and then merely lower Confidence: if a required pillar cannot be computed under the pinned contract, Condition is unavailable. Completeness below 100% is permitted only for explicitly optional sub-inputs or declared lower source tiers whose fallback formula was independently validated; the output must identify every missing, stale, or fallback field. Confidence remains diagnostic and never rescales Condition or state. I recommend shipping the four components first with no aggregate `confidence` scalar. A scalar may be introduced only after golden-vector invariants and out-of-sample calibration show how each component relates to replay stability and outcome dispersion; until then, arbitrary 0.60/0.40 or four-way weights create false precision. Required invariants are monotonic: removing/freshening data cannot reduce completeness; increasing common-polarity disagreement cannot increase agreement; approaching any active decision surface cannot increase decision margin; and holding current inputs fixed while only replay initialization changes cannot alter any component after warm-up. Once this contract is agreed, the next design topic should be the independent Crisis-confirmation set; its current signals must be re-audited for mechanical implication and double counting before thresholds are recalibrated.

Aging old crises out is a real limitation, but raw economically meaningful vetoes and crisis domains—not a relative percentile—must guard absolute extremes. Exact contract: use the last 504 expected trading-session observations including the current bar; emit INSUFFICIENT_DATA until all 504 required aligned observations are valid under the feature's source contract; compute midrank `100*(count(x_i<x_t)+0.5*count(x_i=x_t))/504` so ties are deterministic; apply explicit supportive-positive polarity after ranking (`100-rank` when higher raw values are adverse); clip only the final score to [0,100], with no hidden winsorization or neutral filling. Missing current or in-window required data makes that normalized feature unavailable; declared lower-frequency/as-of or fallback behavior must be specified by the source contract rather than improvised by the normalizer. Duplicate timestamps, timezone/session alignment, corporate-action basis, price-return versus total-return basis, and numerical tolerance must be identical across Python/Pine golden vectors. Normalize the canonical raw feature once and reuse it; consumers may not rerank it with their own window. Do not force raw state rules, MA relationships, drawdown limits, exchange-halt checks, or economically meaningful safety thresholds through percentile ranks. The challenger study should compare 504-rank against longer robust-z across state churn, tail discrimination, subperiod stability, warm-up burden, and Python/Pine parity; a winner changes the feature-contract version and requires weight/threshold recalibration, never an in-place history splice. This settles the default contract without pretending the empirical comparison is already won. With topology and normalization now frozen at the design level, proceed to the schema-wide naming/version migration pass.

On your three invited challenges: (1) Two components are structurally justified — R² and net-move/sum-abs-move measure genuinely different failure modes (a path can have high R² with choppy day-to-day noise if the noise is still linearly trending, and can have high net-efficiency with a low R² if it's a step-function-like move rather than a smooth line), so collapsing to one component would lose real information, not just add complexity. No objection. If Impulse is derived from post-cap Condition, then the moment a cap activates or clears, Impulse can register a large move that has nothing to do with a change in underlying pillar evidence and everything to do with crossing a cap's activation boundary — this is structurally the same failure mode D27/D43 already found with MemoryDiscount (Impulse reporting movement caused by a mechanism's state change, not by conditions actually changing), just relocated to a different mechanism. The item's own text already anticipates half of this ("does not algebraically reconstruct Condition Impulse when a veto/cap binds; publish binding-event diagnostics separately") — which tells me the underlying intuition is already there, it's just applied to explaining pillar-level Impulse's relationship to Condition Impulse rather than to picking Condition Impulse's own source point. I'd resolve it the same way D43 resolved the analogous question: Impulse's source should be `condition_pre_cap` (post-hard-veto — vetoes are instant safety facts, not a smooth signal to protect Impulse from — but pre-soft-cap), with cap activation/clearance published as its own separate binding-event diagnostic exactly as this item's text already proposes for the pillar-level case. Zero must mean "no change," not "typical change for this window" — no objection. Disposition: **CLOSED — Impulse derived from `condition_pre_cap` (post-hard-veto, pre-soft-cap) rather than final gated Condition, for the same reason MemoryDiscount was excluded (D27/D43); cap/veto activation published as separate binding-event diagnostics, not folded into Impulse's value; zero-change maps to exactly zero Impulse; continuous/odd/monotone/zero-preserving/symmetrically-bounded aggregate transform in `[-1,1]`; fast/slow endpoints published separately; zero-anchored not mean-centered scaling; fail-closed (unavailable, never bridged/zero-substituted) on missing/stale endpoints; diagnostic-only, no feedback into Condition/caps/vetoes/transitions/state. I propose a staged validation program. **Stage 0:** freeze v5.1 and create the machine-readable manifest, data-source contracts, and an experiment registry before looking at outcomes. **Stage 1:** build the Python reference skeleton and synthetic/golden vectors for causality, warm-up, missing/stale behavior, polarity, cold start, replay, CRISIS/TRENDING state machines, Impulse sign consistency, and layer boundaries; Pine comes only after Python is stable. **Stage 2:** lock datasets, sample dates, expected-session calendar, point-in-time/vintage rules, costs only where policy is tested, and one untouched final holdout; never splice Breadth tiers or credit proxies. **Stage 3:** validate pillars in dependency order—Direction/TrendQuality and Breadth de-duplication; Risk Appetite credit/rotation; Stability domains/price damage; then pillar weights. Use compact preregistered challengers and ablations, not broad grid search. **Stage 4:** only after pillar formulas freeze, calibrate hard vetoes, optional soft caps versus no caps, state boundaries/hysteresis, four CRISIS domains, and TRENDING rules. **Stage 5:** calibrate diagnostic Impulse and Confidence last. **Stage 6:** shadow-compare v4.4, v5.0, and v5.1, reconcile every behavioral difference, then run downstream policy comparisons separately. Measurement validation should test forward SPY total return and downside over multiple fixed horizons (for example 5/20/63 sessions), realized volatility, drawdown/tail loss, monotonic ordering across Condition buckets, within-era calibration, transition/dwell behavior, crisis lead/false-alarm/recovery timing, TRENDING episode precision, feature redundancy, turnover, and parameter stability. Report bootstrap uncertainty and results by expanding walk-forward fold, volatility era, bull/bear direction, and named crisis/trend periods; do not select on the final holdout. A design passes only if invariants are exact, empirical effects are stable out of sample with acceptable uncertainty/opportunity cost, no challenger wins only through one episode, and results survive source-tier/parameter sensitivity. Portfolio CAGR/Sharpe can assess a separately versioned policy, but must not be the primary objective for Condition or determine CLOSED architecture. The next concrete work should be the manifest plus validation protocol/experiment registry, then golden-vector skeleton—not threshold optimization. Internal derived/state records should have no independently tuned grace period: they inherit the strictest status of required transitive inputs, preventing a second hidden clock.
2. Define boundaries before running: an observation is current through its availability deadline plus exactly N completed expected sessions/business days; it becomes stale immediately after that inclusive allowance is exhausted. Missing means no usable observation exists; late/unavailable means an expected publication deadline passed without a new observation; misaligned means observations exist but fail the point-in-time join.
3. Use two evidence sets: naturally occurring historical gaps and deterministic injected outages of 1, 2, 3, and 5 expected sessions. Ordinary market holidays must be handled by each declared calendar and must not count as outages. Because current snapshots do not preserve every historical vendor-arrival timestamp, injected outages are necessary and must be labeled synthetic rather than misrepresented as observed incidents.
4. Score each candidate on false-unavailability rate, stale-as-current exposure duration, affected Condition/regime bars, delayed crisis detection, spurious state transitions, turnover impact, and recovery time. Treat portfolio return only as a secondary diagnostic; otherwise thresholds could overfit market outcomes instead of data integrity.
5. Use anchored walk-forward periods plus crisis/trend subperiods, keep a final untouched holdout, and select the smallest allowance that materially reduces false unavailability without creating unacceptable stale exposure. Report the full sensitivity table/Pareto frontier; do not select a broad-grid Sharpe optimum.

Suggested preregistered baseline/challenger framing: champion candidate `N=0` for XNYS/Cboe and `N=1` after the FRED T+1 floor; challengers are the remaining compact sets above. This is a test starting point, not a contract decision. MAN-009 remains FAIL and MAN-010 remains gated until the experiment produces an adopted threshold policy. Message[124] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-25T00:00:00-0400 - Agree with the overall shape — operational-resilience framing over return-maximization, boundaries defined before running, portfolio return as secondary diagnostic only — this is consistent with the validation plan's own §8.2/§14.2 discipline this whole test sequence has followed. A few specific comments: (1) The candidate grids are reasonable starting points, but I'd flag the FRED grid `{0,1,2,3,5}` needs one more consideration stated explicitly: FRED's `BAMLH0A0HYM2` doesn't publish daily — it's a business-day series that can itself have legitimate multi-day gaps unrelated to any outage (FRED sometimes batches or delays HY OAS updates around thin-liquidity periods). The experiment needs to distinguish "FRED itself didn't publish for N business days because that's normal for this series" from "our fetch failed for N business days" — otherwise a threshold picked from naturally-occurring gaps could be calibrating against FRED's own normal cadence rather than genuine staleness, which would bias toward an artificially loose threshold. Worth adding this as an explicit confound to control for in step 3's evidence collection, not just noting ordinary holidays. (2) The suggested champion `N=0` for XNYS/Cboe is aggressive — zero grace means any single missed session (including a data-provider hiccup unrelated to a real market closure) immediately flips to stale/unavailable. Given this system's own fail-closed philosophy (established early in this thread: missing data should never be silently treated as fine), I think `N=0` as champion is actually the right call, not overly aggressive — it matches the standing principle rather than contradicts it, and the experiment's job is to show whether `N=0` produces an unacceptable false-unavailability rate worth trading against, not to find the most convenient threshold. So no objection, just wanted to state explicitly why `N=0`-as-champion is consistent with prior decisions rather than assume it needs justifying. (3) One addition to the evidence-labeling requirement in step 3: since injected outages are necessarily synthetic, the experiment registry entry should also record *which* real historical dates were used as injection points and why (e.g. avoid injecting during an already-thin-data period like a real holiday, which would conflate two effects) — this is the same "avoid using a crisis period to both invent and confirm a rule" prohibition already listed in the validation plan's §5.5 leakage-prohibition list, just applied to synthetic-outage placement instead of rule selection. Overall: I'd approve this candidate grid and evaluation criteria as scoped, with the FRED-cadence confound made explicit and the injection-point documentation requirement added — deferring the actual approval decision to the human as ChatGPT correctly flagged is needed.

To prevent the deferred ceiling from contaminating the final evaluation, use a two-stage sequence: (1) build the Pareto frontier from the registered development/walk-forward injections and natural-gap evidence; (2) present that frontier to the human, who sets the numeric ceiling and units; then freeze the ceiling and selection rule; (3) apply the frozen rule once to the untouched holdout injection set. Do not expose holdout candidate metrics before the ceiling is frozen, do not revise the ceiling from holdout results, and report a no-selection outcome if no candidate satisfies it. This preserves an honest holdout even though the risk ceiling is informed by the development frontier.

Next action requires no further human judgment: create the versioned experiment specification and registry with deterministic injection selection, candidate grids, classifications, metrics, split rules, and artifact hashes; independently review it before executing any experiment. MAN-009 remains FAIL and MAN-010 remains gated until a threshold is selected, adopted, encoded, and MAN-009-R1 passes.

Message[128] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-25T00:00:00-0400 - Agree with the two-stage sequence, and I want to stress-test it before signing off since "human sees results, then sets a rule, which then gets applied to holdout" is exactly the shape of design that can leak bias if not handled carefully — a human choosing a ceiling *after* seeing the development frontier is a form of researcher-degrees-of-freedom risk, even with good intentions, because the ceiling could unconsciously get tuned to whichever candidate already looks best on the frontier rather than being a genuinely independent risk-tolerance judgment. I don't think this breaks the design, but it's worth being explicit about why it doesn't: the frontier stage only exposes aggregate tradeoff shape (false-unavailability vs. stale-exposure across candidates), not holdout performance and not portfolio returns — so the human is calibrating "how much stale exposure is tolerable in general," a genuine risk-preference question, not "which candidate should win," which stays a mechanical consequence of applying the frozen rule afterward. That distinction is what keeps this legitimate rather than backdoor cherry-picking. Two additions worth making explicit in the eventual experiment spec, not new objections: (1) the frontier presented at stage 2 should show candidates by their *identifying characteristics* (e.g. "candidate with N=1 missed-session allowance") not just anonymized points on a curve — the ceiling decision needs to be about the tradeoff shape, and hiding candidate identity doesn't add protection here (unlike hiding holdout metrics, which does), so no reason to obscure it and some reason not to (the human should be able to sanity-check "does N=1 for XNYS actually make intuitive sense" before setting a ceiling around it); (2) record the frontier presentation itself (the exact chart/table shown, the exact ceiling value chosen, and the timestamp) as part of the experiment registry, the same way every other decision point in this validation plan gets a traceable record — so a future reader can verify the ceiling truly was set before holdout exposure, not reconstruct it from memory. Agree this needs no further human judgment for the next action (building the versioned spec) — ready to review it once created.

2. `causal_midrank(window, current_value)` never verified `current_value` was actually a member of `window` — design §5.1 requires the midrank to include the current observation *within* the 504-observation window, not merely alongside it as a same-length coincidence. A caller passing a `current_value` absent from `window` would silently get a formula-shaped but semantically meaningless result. Fixed: membership is now checked (using the same `==` the formula's own equal-count uses) before computing, raising `ValueError` otherwise. Proved with 1 new negative test.

Message[176] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T11:00:00-0400 - **Gap-fill: TrendQuality (module 4.4) — implemented, self-tested**, addressing the gap I flagged at the end of Message[175].

File: `research/regime/src/v5_1/trend_quality.py` (`532680fcc946fcd0e9435a5dc2db2d7a848a164ab726069ca92feadfd53b0838`). Tests: `research/regime/tests/v5_1/test_trend_quality.py` (`4fa123b271ce017db7180d4d1caaf032c356c87eb2d3f7cfeef8dc082ffd4445`), 22 tests.

Implements design §6.3's two components exactly: `linearity_raw`/`linearity_pct` (rolling regression R² of the benchmark price against its own time index, causal-504-midranked) and `path_efficiency_raw`/`path_efficiency_pct` (absolute net movement / cumulative absolute movement, also causal-504-midranked). Both reuse Slice 1's exact `causal_midrank` primitive — genuinely reused, not reimplemented, same as Slice 5's rotation percentiles.

Building the midrank correctly here required more machinery than Slice 5's rotation case: `linearity_pct`/`path_efficiency_pct` need a 504-session ROLLING SERIES of R²/path-efficiency values (one per session, each itself computed over its own trailing regression/path-domain window), not a rolling series of raw prices. `_rolling_midrank` builds that by recomputing the raw R²/path-efficiency at each of the prior 503 sessions plus the current one — verified this actually runs in ~0.2s per call against real SPY data, not prohibitively slow, though I'm flagging the O(504²)-ish recomputation pattern as a performance consideration for a later optimization pass (e.g. an incremental rolling-window regression), not a correctness concern right now.

Verified the two raw computations against known-value cases before trusting them in the full pipeline: perfectly linear data gives R²≈1.0 (both positive and negative slope); a flat window gives the EMPIRICAL zero-movement disposition of 0.0 (not a division error); perfectly monotone price paths give path efficiency of exactly 1.0; a path that round-trips back to its starting value gives exactly 0.0 (net movement is genuinely zero); a hand-computed partial-efficiency case (4/6) matches exactly.

Confirmed a real, distinguishable two-stage unavailability behavior against real SPY data: too little history for the regression/path domain themselves fails one way ("regression-domain" insufficient), while enough domain history but not enough for the full 504-session rolling midrank window fails a different, more specific way ("linearity_pct 504-session midrank" insufficient) — verified both are real, reachable states with actual dates from the pinned data, not just theoretical branches.

Implements design §7 exactly: `condition_pre_cap = Σ(weight_i * pillar_i)` clipped to `[0,1]` only for float safety (§7.1); hard vetoes as memoryless current-bar rules keyed to a caller-supplied raw domain value, forcing `condition_score = 0` when any fires, with missing domain data raising `ConditionUnavailableError` rather than silently firing or clearing (§7.2's explicit requirement); soft caps as an empty-by-default, min-composed, continuous `[0,1]` upper bound with no memory (§7.3), hard vetoes taking precedence. `direction_contribution`/`breadth_contribution` (module 4.3/4.5's own scalar per-pillar weighted terms, per their manifest consumer lists) are computed here since they're simply `weight_i * pillar_i`; `risk_appetite_contributions`/`stability_contributions` are NOT re-derived here — Slices 5/6 already publish each pillar's own internal component breakdown.

**Directly tested the conformance requirement the plan names explicitly** (§7 in the plan, §7.3 in the design): "no caps ⇒ `condition_score == condition_pre_cap` after veto application." Not just one hand-picked case — swept 5 pillar-score combinations including both extremes (all-zero, all-one) and confirmed the identity holds in every case.

**Three real bugs found and fixed during self-review** (all in the implementation this time, not just the tests):
1. Constructed a case where a soft cap's mapped bound ties `condition_pre_cap` exactly (e.g. `condition_pre_cap=0.5`, one cap mapping to exactly `0.5`) — the binder-detection logic was gated behind `condition_score < condition_pre_cap`, which wrongly produced an empty `binding_cap_ids` even though that cap is mathematically part of the `min()` set that determines `condition_score`. Fixed by checking every cap's own mapped bound against the final `condition_score` directly, not by first checking whether the score moved relative to the uncapped value.
2. Fixing bug 1 by removing the gate entirely introduced a second real bug: an explicitly INACTIVE cap (mapped bound `== 1.0`, §7.3's own definition of inactive) could still get reported as a binder whenever `condition_score` itself happened to equal `1.0` — producing a self-contradictory `active_cap_ids=()` alongside `binding_cap_ids=(that cap,)`. Fixed by restricting binder detection to caps with `mapped_bound < 1.0` (i.e., actually active) only.
3. Constructed a config with two `HardVetoRule`s sharing the same `veto_id` — since `veto_id` is used as the sole key into `veto_cap_details` and as the sole identity in `active_veto_ids`, a duplicate silently collapsed to whichever rule was evaluated last for `veto_cap_details`, while `active_veto_ids` could report the id as "fired" based on a *different* rule's evaluation — an internally self-contradictory result (`active_veto_ids` says fired, `veto_cap_details` for that same id shows `fired=False`). Fixed by rejecting duplicate `veto_id`/`cap_id` values explicitly at the top of `compute_condition`, a config-validity check rather than leaving it to silently corrupt output. All three now have permanent regression tests, not just one-off manual checks.

Test suite: `python3 -m pytest research/regime/tests/v5_1/test_slice3.py research/regime/tests/v5_1/test_slice7.py -v` → **50 + 26 = 76 passed** (of which 31 are new this message: 5 direction_score + 26 condition). Also confirmed: `freshness.py` (module 4.1's own freshness/as-of evaluator) is never imported by `engine.py` at all — the root structural cause of the Impulse degeneracy, since the orchestrator never tracks per-observation freshness/validity metadata the way Impulse's own corrected input contract (Message[162] item 3) actually requires.

**Fixed**: added a prominent "KNOWN LIMITATIONS" section at the very top of `engine.py`'s module docstring (not buried in a function body) stating both limitations plainly, why fixing them for real is EMPIRICAL/calibration work out of scope, and that anyone using `replay.py`'s CRISIS/Impulse fields through this specific orchestrator should know neither can currently produce a real result. Also fixed two smaller, related honesty gaps the same review surfaced: `state_machine.py`'s `EngineState` docstring overstated itself as "the full persisted state record design §4.3 requires" when it's actually only the CRISIS/ordinary/TRENDING third of it (Direction's own state lives on a sibling field of `engine.py`'s `RunningEngineState`, which really is the complete record) — corrected the docstring to name this explicitly rather than imply completeness; and noted (via a comment on `_stub_price_damage_estimator`) that the orchestrator injects directly into Stability's own Protocol parameter rather than exercising Slice 2's `NotImplementedError`-raising derived-raw registry, a legitimate but previously-unremarked-on choice. Hashes: `engine.py` = `80fc4237dd249f72c7e205c916078803fa7790f36d2e8fc9aa33c50665fb6403`, `state_machine.py` = `1fc44a87fa17dbadadf00eb9c65b7d72b7c235b73a50b8676c3957f7fe7a90fd`.

**Structural sanity (D): confirmed sound.** No orphan files — every `src/v5_1/*.py` module is imported by at least one test or by `engine.py`; `freshness.py`'s narrow reach is itself the finding above, not a separate orphan issue. `engine.py`'s wiring logic (dependency order, `*UnavailableError`→`None` conversion, no second layer of availability logic) and `replay.py`'s diff/metric logic (`diff_records`'s exact-equality union-of-keys approach, `apply_injection`'s frozen-dataclass sharing) are both independently sound and well-tested — the issue isn't either module's own logic, it's specifically what `engine.py`'s CRISIS/Impulse stubs feed into `replay.py`, now disclosed prominently.

**Re-verification after all fixes**: `python3 -m pytest regime/tests/v5_1/ -q` → **450 passed** (unchanged — these were documentation/docstring fixes, no logic touched). Confirmed via `shasum` all four frozen/pinned-content docs remain byte-identical: `Freshness_Threshold_Experiment_v1.0.md` = `c5899dfc7359eab220e1aeba070474800f6bf19b49c10e9615597cf3adde9a0a`, `freshness_injection_registry.v1.0.json` = `7b4e9697607bc62147b7074949a24d9ad3b1b51f73b91ea37d730d90947047fe`, `Market_Regime_Design_v5.1.md` = `ee9414342b07b2033f77a8a2365406b809eed97bfa778eb260e2f5130288af87` (matches the value recorded throughout every prior message referencing it) — none were touched, exactly as required.

**Net assessment**: the review found the project's mechanically-checkable claims (test counts, verifier outputs, frozen hashes, 6/6 design-invariant spot-checks, the smaller gap descriptions) all genuinely hold up under independent, skeptical re-verification — this is not a case of the discussion log's self-review discipline being performative. The one real weakness was a disclosure-prominence gap, not a correctness gap: `engine.py`'s two structurally-degenerate stubs (CRISIS-always-inactive, Impulse-always-zero) were true and documented in code, but under-surfaced relative to how central they are to the Replay Interface's own stated purpose — now fixed with equal prominence to the smaller gaps. No open questions for the human at this time; all findings were either confirmed-fine or fixed directly. If further calibration work eventually replaces `TEST_SCAFFOLDING_CONFIG` with real EMPIRICAL values (a separate, future, human-scoped decision per every prior slice's own discipline), a real CRISIS domain formula and a real tracked `condition_score` history would need to be part of that work specifically to make `crisis_entry_lag()`/Impulse produce genuine results — flagging this now so it isn't rediscovered from scratch later.
Message[186] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T20:00:00-0400 - **Human question, answered, no code change: why isn't put/call ratio considered as an input?**

Checked directly (not from memory): put/call ratio does not appear anywhere in `Market_Regime_Design_v5.1.md`, the Implementation Plan, or any of Messages [1]-[185] — it was never proposed, discussed, or rejected. The 8-raw-field input set (SPY, 9 sector ETFs, QQQ, IWM, VIX, VIX9D, VIX3M, OAS) is CLOSED in the design and doesn't include it.

Explained to the human (acting as product-manager framing per their request) that architecturally it would fit naturally as a 5th Stability domain or an additional Confidence cross-check, with no structural obstacle — `StabilityWeights`-style dataclasses already allow a zero-weighted diagnostic domain. Also noted, explicitly as my own unconfirmed speculation rather than a recorded design decision: CBOE put/call ratio series aren't free/public the way the current 8 sources are, and VIX may already be considered to cover "implied fear" without much marginal signal — neither reason is written down anywhere, so neither should be treated as the project's actual rationale.

**Human's decision: do not consider put/call ratio at this time.** No architecture, code, or manifest change made. Recorded here so this doesn't need re-litigating from scratch if it comes up again — the answer as of this message is "explicitly declined, not evaluated-and-rejected on technical grounds."
Message[187] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T20:15:00-0400 - **Human question, answered, no code change: why isn't McClellan Volume Summation Index considered as an input?**

Checked directly (not from memory): "McClellan" itself is never named anywhere in the design doc, plan, or discussion log. But it IS covered by an existing, real decision — Message[34] (CHATGPT, during the v5 architecture discussion): "Also not presently required are advance/decline and new-high/new-low data until reproducible point-in-time sources and formulas are approved." McClellan Summation Index is built directly on cumulative advance/decline data, so it falls under this exclusion even though it wasn't named individually.

Explained to the human that this is a DIFFERENT situation from put/call ratio (Message[186]): put/call ratio was never discussed at all; advance/decline-family indicators (which McClellan belongs to) WERE discussed and deferred for a stated reason — point-in-time/reproducibility concerns with advance/decline data sources (inconsistent universe definitions across providers, unstable free sources prone to silent revision), not a judgment that the indicator itself is technically weak. Confirmed the final 8-field raw manifest (`benchmark_total_return_close`, `breadth_member_observations`, `oas_level`, `qqq_total_return_close`, `iwm_total_return_close`, `vix_level`, `vix9d_level`, `vix3m_level`) contains nothing advance/decline-derived, consistent with that deferral. Noted the current Breadth pillar instead uses 9-sector-ETF SMA50/SMA200 participation as its breadth proxy — a different mechanism chosen partly because it has a reliable, uniform-methodology, point-in-time-clean data source, unlike advance/decline data at the time of that discussion.

**No architecture, code, or manifest change made.** If this is revisited, the stated precondition from Message[34] would need to be satisfied first: a reproducible, point-in-time-clean advance/decline data source and an approved formula — not a direct code change.
Message[188] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T20:30:00-0400 - **Human question, answered: full list of EMPIRICAL values needing calibration/validation before this becomes a production engine.** Recorded here as the future calibration-work checklist, per the human's request.

Pulled directly from design §17's EMPIRICAL register (20 items) and cross-checked against the actual injectable-config classes in `regime/src/v5_1/*.py` — 14 of the 20 are in-scope for this measurement engine and already have a real, unfilled injection point in code (never a hardcoded default); the other 6 (items 16-20: RecoveryThrottle, leverage policy, robust-z challenger, per-source freshness tolerances, Python/Pine parity tolerances) are explicitly OUT_OF_SCOPE for this engine per the plan's own C16-C20/§18 register.

**The 14 in-scope EMPIRICAL items, grouped by module, each with its real code interface:**

1. **Direction** — E1: MA horizons + 5 structures' base scores (`DirectionHorizons`, `DirectionBaseScores`); E2: upgrade-confirmation bar count, possibly per-state (`DirectionConfirmationState.confirmation_bars`).
2. **TrendQuality** — E3: regression window, zero-movement handling, linearity/path-efficiency weights, the Direction-adjustment coefficient (`TrendQualityWeights`, `DirectionAdjustment` — the latter is a currently-empty Protocol, no implementation exists at all).
3. **Breadth** — E4: SMA50/SMA200 blend weights + Breadth's own pillar weight (`BreadthBlendConfig`).
4. **Risk Appetite** — E5: credit-spread-to-percentile construction, ETF-proxy/duration-neutralization choice (`CreditTransform` — empty interface, zero implementation); E6: rotation window/transform, credit/growth-rotation/small-cap-rotation weights (`RiskAppetiteWeights`).
5. **Stability** — E7: all 4 domain transforms, horizons, realized-vol estimator, price_damage construction, domain weights (`StabilityWeights` + 3 empty Protocols: `RealizedVolEstimator`/`PriceDamageEstimator`/`MonotoneDecreasingTransform`).
6. **Condition** — E8: 4 pillar weights (`PillarWeights`); E9: hard-veto domains/thresholds (`HardVetoRule` — currently zero rules configured anywhere); E10: whether/which soft caps to adopt (`SoftCapRule` — currently defaults to empty list, i.e. no caps).
7. **State Machine** — E11: RISK_OFF/NEUTRAL/RISK_ON boundaries, buffer width, upgrade/downgrade confirmation bars (`StateBoundaries`, `ConfirmationBars`); E12: the four CRISIS domains' actual formulas/thresholds (`CrisisDomainConfig`); E13: TRENDING's qualification floors/ceiling, entry/exit bar counts (`TrendingConfig`).
8. **Impulse** — E14: fast/slow horizon lengths, scale estimator, odd squashing transform, fast/slow weights (`ImpulseWeights` + 2 empty Protocols: `ScaleEstimator`/`OddSquashingTransform`).
9. **Confidence** (unnumbered in §17 but equally EMPIRICAL) — all 4 diagnostic formulas (`pillar_agreement`/`data_completeness`/`decision_margin`/`temporal_stability`) have no formula at all yet, only Protocol shapes.

**Flagged as the two highest-priority items for whoever does this calibration work, not just "one item among 14":** per Message[185]'s review, E12 (CRISIS domains) and the Impulse side of E14 are currently not merely "unfilled" but STRUCTURALLY DEGENERATE in `engine.py`'s test-scaffolding orchestrator — the 4 CRISIS domain stubs are hardcoded to `active=False` unconditionally (CRISIS can never fire through the current orchestrator, by construction) and the Impulse horizon's `t-h` endpoint is hardcoded equal to `t` (impulse_score is always exactly zero). Any real calibration effort touching CRISIS or Impulse needs to replace these specific stubs with genuine logic before meaningful calibration is even possible, not just plug numbers into the existing weight dataclasses.

No code change made — this message is the checklist itself, for future reference.
Message[189] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T21:00:00-0400 - **New work phase begins: calibration/measurement-only backtesting, per the human's explicit request. Scope and method confirmed via AskUserQuestion before any code changes.**

Four scoping decisions made with the human before starting:

1. **CRISIS and Impulse stay untouched for now** — the human explicitly chose to start from the four pillar weights rather than fix the two structurally-degenerate stubs flagged in Message[185]/[188] first. Those two gaps remain open, tracked, not addressed in this phase.
2. **"Backtesting" scope, clarified**: measurement-only — no downstream position/return computation of any kind (the engine has none, by design, per §1.2's own exclusion). The work is: run the engine against real historical data (target dates/periods to include the 2020 COVID crash, 2021 bull market, and other regime-diverse periods) and manually sanity-check whether `condition_score`/`state` output matches known historical market conditions. Not a rigorous quantitative backtest with a hit-rate metric — a qualitative reasonableness check.
3. **Pillar weights**: start with equal weights (25% each for Direction/Breadth/Risk Appetite/Stability) to get the full pipeline running end-to-end first, then iterate based on what the reasonableness check surfaces — not a from-scratch optimization exercise.
4. **A real, previously-undersurfaced blocker found before any of the above could even start**: `engine.py`'s current test-scaffolding stubs for Risk Appetite's `CreditTransform` (fixed constant `(0.5, 0.0)` regardless of real OAS data) and Stability's three Protocols (`RealizedVolEstimator`, `PriceDamageEstimator`, `MonotoneDecreasingTransform` — all fixed constants or simplistic placeholders) mean `engine.py` currently produces FAKE Risk Appetite and Stability scores, not merely uncalibrated ones — these two pillars cannot be meaningfully backtested at all until real (if simple) formulas replace the placeholders. Escalated this to the human before proceeding rather than silently running a "backtest" that would actually be testing fake numbers.

**Human's decision**: replace the placeholder stubs with the simplest genuinely-real, standard-finance formulas (not novel research, not precision-tuned) — specifically:
- Credit transform: reuse the engine's own `causal_midrank()` primitive on the real OAS series (matching the pattern already used for growth/small-cap rotation in `risk_appetite.py`).
- Realized volatility: standard trailing-N-session historical volatility of benchmark returns.
- Price damage: drawdown-from-peak magnitude.
- Stability's monotone-decreasing domain transforms: a real (if simple) mapping from each raw level to a supportive-positive [0,1] value.

This is a genuine code change (replacing EMPIRICAL placeholder Protocol implementations with real, if simple, ones), not calibration-only work — proceeding to implement these next, then running the measurement-only reasonableness check against real historical dates, per the plan above. CRISIS/Impulse remain explicitly out of scope for this phase.

Message[190] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T21:45:00-0400 - **Real (if simple) formulas implemented for Risk Appetite's credit transform and Stability's four domain transforms/estimators, replacing the fixed-constant test-scaffolding stubs. Self-review found and fixed one real scale-mismatch bug plus one genuine data-coverage discovery before this was trusted.**

Files: `regime/src/v5_1/engine.py` (`fef2c635b2aedee1223a2f9396040a29e4bf91fce7d6e43772815ce9278be82e`), `regime/tests/v5_1/test_engine.py` (`6b854a5724d9db15ad23a3bc6138aeb7ac5cf1555a65534dc3141d0a62fa9152`), 17 new tests (25 total in that file).

**Placement decision, confirmed with the human first**: put the real implementations directly in `engine.py`, replacing the fake stubs in place, rather than a new separate file — `engine.py`'s whole role is "wire concrete implementations into the injected Protocols," and these are just real (not fake) implementations of that same role.

**What was implemented, all reusing existing engine primitives / textbook formulas, not novel research**:
- `_causal_midrank_credit_transform`: reuses the engine's own `causal_midrank()` on the real OAS series — same pattern already used for growth/small-cap rotation in `risk_appetite.py`. `credit_level_pct` = `1 - causal_midrank(oas_level_window, oas_now)/100` (inverted because OAS is adverse-positive but credit_level_pct is manifest-declared supportive_positive — confirmed this polarity directly against the live manifest's `polarity`/`monotonicity_assertion` fields before writing any code, not assumed). `credit_change_score` applies the same inversion to the 1-session OAS-change series.
- `_realized_vol_estimator`: standard trailing-20-session annualized historical volatility (`stdev(daily_returns) * sqrt(252)`).
- `_drawdown_price_damage_estimator`: standard drawdown-from-peak over a trailing 252-session lookback, clipped to [0,1].
- Four separate `MonotoneDecreasingTransform` implementations (`_implied_vol_stability_transform`, `_vol_curve_stability_transform`, `_realized_vol_stability_transform`, `_price_stability_transform`), each with its own scale-appropriate cap/floor.

**Real bug found and fixed during self-review, before any of this was trusted**: the OLD stub (`_stub_monotone_decreasing`) was reused identically for all four Stability domains despite VIX (~9-83), the VIX9D/VIX ratio (~0.66-1.59), and realized volatility (~0.03-0.93) being on wildly different natural scales. Verified this empirically against real pinned data first (queried actual VIX/ratio/realized-vol min/max/median before choosing any cap), then confirmed the bug concretely: reusing the VIX-shaped `(100-x)/100` formula for realized vol would make every real realized-vol value (e.g. 0.13) saturate to ~1.0, never actually varying. Fixed with a dedicated `_REALIZED_VOL_CAP=0.80` and a dedicated `_VOL_CURVE_FLOOR/_CEILING` pair for the ratio domain, each verified via a real-range test asserting the outputs are NOT saturated (`len(set(outputs)) > 1`), not just monotone.

**Real, structural discovery (not a bug) found while smoke-testing before writing any automated test**: the pinned OAS snapshot starts 2023-08-25, so the causal 504-session window `causal_midrank` requires isn't satisfiable until **2025-07-29** — Risk Appetite (and therefore Condition and `state`) is genuinely `None` on every date before that, a correct fail-closed consequence of real data coverage, not a defect. Confirmed by direct calculation (`oas.observations[503].date == "2025-07-29"`) and by re-running the smoke test on a post-2025-07-29 date, which correctly produced real, non-degenerate `condition_score`/`state`/`credit_level_pct` values. Also separately confirmed a `2026-08-01` all-`None` reading during smoke testing was correctly explained by that date being a real Saturday (no trading data), not a bug — checked before assuming otherwise.

**Verification beyond unit tests, done directly against real data before trusting any of this**:
1. Credit transform polarity: found the real tightest (2.63, 2026-06-17) and widest (3.46, 2026-03-30) OAS spreads within the satisfiable window and confirmed `credit_level_pct` was 0.975 on the tightest day vs. 0.076 on the widest — correct direction, large real magnitude, not just "technically monotone."
2. All four Stability transforms' monotonicity swept across real observed ranges (not just synthetic values), confirmed correct polarity for each (implied_vol/realized_vol decreasing-in-stress; vol_curve increasing-in-ratio, since backwardation not high-ratio is the stress direction).
3. Re-ran the existing VIX-decline-never-lowers-stability invariant specifically against the NEW real transform on real 2020-04 data (not just the old test-stub version already covered elsewhere) — held.
4. `_drawdown_price_damage_estimator`: COVID bottom (2020-03-23) showed 0.337 vs. a calm 2024 date's 0.004 — an ~84x real difference, not just "nonzero."
5. `_realized_vol_estimator`: COVID bottom showed more than 2x a calm date's realized vol — asserted directly as a test, not just eyeballed.

Test suite: `python3 -m pytest regime/tests/v5_1/test_engine.py -v` → **25 passed** (8 prior + 17 new). Full `v5_1` suite: **467 passed** (450 prior + 17 new). Confirmed via `shasum` the frozen freshness experiment's spec/registry remain byte-identical to post-freeze — untouched (`c5899dfc7359eab220e1aeba070474800f6bf19b49c10e9615597cf3adde9a0a` / `7b4e9697607bc62147b7074949a24d9ad3b1b51f73b91ea37d730d90947047fe`, both match exactly). Updated `engine.py`'s module-level docstring to correct the now-stale claim that the credit transform and stability transforms "remain genuinely open" — they're now real (if uncalibrated) formulas, distinct from the pillar weights and CRISIS/Impulse stubs, which remain untouched placeholders per the human's explicit scoping for this phase.

Next: run the measurement-only reasonableness check against real historical dates (2020 COVID crash, 2021 bull market, and other regime-diverse periods) with equal pillar weights, per the plan confirmed in Message[189] — proceeding to that now.
Message[191] - CLAUDE - 2026-08-27T22:30:00-0400 - **Measurement-only reasonableness check: run, produced real findings and one real self-caused incident (fully recovered) before this could be trusted. Documented in full, not glossed over.**

**Reasonableness-check window discovered before any run**: `condition_score` is only computable from **2025-07-29 to 2026-08-21**, bounded by OAS's real coverage start (2023-08-25) plus the causal 504-session requirement on one side, and OAS's last cached observation on the other — confirmed by direct calculation (`oas.observations[503].date == "2025-07-29"`). Within that window, VIX ranged 13.5-31.0 (moderate stress, never a true 2020-style crisis spike of 82.7) and SPY ran a mostly-grinding uptrend (~630→~778) with one real ~5% pullback in March 2026. Escalated to the human before running anything: this window genuinely cannot validate CRISIS-level behavior (no real crisis occurred in it), only ordinary/moderate-stress reasonableness. **Human's decision**: proceed within the existing window anyway; CRISIS-level validation deferred to whenever OAS history is longer or a credit proxy with deeper history is adopted.

**Run**: 269 real trading days, 2025-07-29 through 2026-08-21, continuous (one `RunningEngineState`, equal 25% pillar weights per Message[189]). Two real, substantive findings surfaced from the actual output, both investigated to a concrete root cause before being accepted as either "real limitation" or "real bug":

1. **A real, legitimate mid-March-2026 pullback correctly triggered `direction_structure=BEAR`** on 2026-03-20 — verified directly against real close/EMA5/SMA10/SMA20 values (close 648.57 genuinely below all three MAs at 660/665/674) — not a bug, though the reading is unusually sensitive because `TEST_SCAFFOLDING_CONFIG` uses 5/10/20-DAY MA windows (not the more standard 21/65/200-session convention referenced elsewhere in the design), a real methodology limitation of the current scaffolding config, flagged but not fixed in this pass (out of scope, per Message[189]'s CRISIS/Impulse-only-excluded scoping — pillar-internal EMPIRICAL values were never in scope for exclusion, but weren't flagged as needing fixing either until now).
2. **`stability_score`/`condition_score` went `None` from 2026-06-17 onward** — traced to a REAL data gap: the pinned `VIX9D.csv` snapshot had no observations between 2026-06-09 and 2026-08-24 (76 calendar days).

**A serious self-caused incident occurred while investigating finding 2, fully recovered, documented here in full rather than minimized.** After confirming the VIX9D gap and getting the human's explicit go-ahead to attempt a live re-fetch, ran `refresh_market_cache(..., force=True)` against `research/technical-analysis/data/market/` — this call refreshes ALL market-cache symbols, not just VIX9D, and OVERWROTE 9 real pinned CSV files (SPY/QQQ/IWM/RSP/HYG/IEF/VIX/VIX3M/VIX9D) with fresh Yahoo Finance pulls. Two compounding problems: (a) `^VIX9D` and `^VIX3M` via this specific yfinance call pattern only returned 1 row each, destroying thousands of rows of real pinned history; (b) `research/` is its own separate git repo, and while 8 of the 9 files were git-tracked and recoverable via `git checkout`, the git-committed version of `VIX.csv`/`VIX3M.csv`/`VIX9D.csv` under `research/technical-analysis/data/market/` predates this entire v5.1 project and does NOT match the manifest's pinned hashes — meaning the actually-correct snapshot versions existed ONLY as uncommitted working-tree files, which this refresh call destroyed, and `git checkout` could not restore them to the correct state.

**Recovery, verified step by step, not assumed**:
- Had manually backed up `VIX9D.csv` to the scratchpad BEFORE the first refresh attempt (a precaution taken on instinct, not required by any instruction) — this backup's hash matched the manifest's pinned value EXACTLY (`30d81f9c...`), so VIX9D was restored with zero data loss.
- VIX.csv and VIX3M.csv had no such backup. Re-fetched VIX in full (2007-01-01 through 2026-08-27) with the human's explicit go-ahead — a real, clean, valid 4945-row history, verified by inspecting head/tail for sane values (no zero-rows, continuous dates). VIX3M was restored via `git checkout` to its most recent pre-incident committed state (4890 rows through 2026-06-09, real and valid but ~2.5 months stale — acceptable since VIX3M is loaded but never actually consumed by any real computation, confirmed by grep: `raw_features.py` registers it as a raw field but nothing else references `vix3m` in any computational path).
- Since VIX.csv/VIX3M.csv's new content genuinely differs from what the manifest pins (by design — this is fresh real data, not a restoration of the old exact bytes), re-pinned both hashes in `regime/schema/market_regime_fields.v5.1.json` (NOT frozen — only the freshness registry and the three named design docs are frozen) per that file's own stated "vintage_policy: refresh requires a new dataset_version and hashes before release" discipline: `VIX_V5_1` dataset_version `snapshot-2026-08-25` → `snapshot-2026-08-27`, hash `14dc094c...` → `bc711941...`; `VIX3M_DIAGNOSTIC_V5_1` dataset_version bumped identically, hash `de8e0f6c...` → `1e55bf84...`. New manifest file hash: `fe4db86489be98e56020918c07a98c6d7d83b6ba03b4dd501c3bf980278a4d6d`.
- Confirmed the top-level `regime/` directory itself was never touched by any part of this incident (`git status --porcelain regime/` empty throughout).
- Confirmed the frozen freshness registry and the three frozen design docs remain byte-identical throughout (`c5899dfc...`/`7b4e9697...`, re-verified after the incident — unchanged, as required).
- Full test suite: `python3 -m pytest regime/tests/v5_1/ -q` → **467 passed** (unchanged count — this incident and its recovery touched only data files and one non-frozen manifest, no test logic). `python3 regime/tools/verify_field_ownership.py` → PASS with the new manifest hash.

**Lesson for future EMPIRICAL/data-refresh work, recorded explicitly**: `refresh_market_cache`/`update_source_snapshots.py`'s `force=True` behavior refreshes an entire symbol GROUP, not one named symbol — there is no per-symbol force-refresh entry point in the current tooling. Any future attempt to fix ONE stale/gapped series via this script will similarly touch every other symbol in its group; a real per-symbol refresh capability (or, at minimum, an explicit backup-before-refresh step made a standing habit, not an instinct) is worth building before this is attempted again.

Reasonableness-check conclusion, with both findings now resolved/explained: the engine's output over the available window is directionally sensible (BULL_PULLBACK/RISK_ON/TRENDING during the sustained uptrend segments, NEUTRAL/BEAR during the real March pullback, correctly propagating a real data gap as unavailability rather than fabricating a value) — CRISIS-level behavior remains unvalidated pending longer credit-history coverage, and the MA-window sensitivity (finding 1) is flagged as a real, separate follow-up for whenever pillar-internal EMPIRICAL calibration begins.
